ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u4	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The operation of raw materials has always been associated with a series of threats to mining plants and miners. Data from the Higher Mining Authority indicate that in 2017 there were 2078 different accidents in the entire Polish mining industry, including 15 fatal accidents. The last available report of the Higher Mining Authority shows that in Polish mining the number of accidents is steadily decreasing. However, there are still dangers underground. Risks are increasing due to the increasing depth of the exploitation of minerals, increased concentration of extraction and operational events. The tragic shock occurred on 5 May this year in the mine Zofiówka belonging to Jastrzębska Spółka Węglowa. The rescue was extremely difficult. Therefore, I ask the Minister to answer the following questions: How does the Ministry intend to increase the level of security in mines? How many inspections did WUG perform in 2017 in underground mining plants? Did WUG control the Zofiówka mine in 2017 and 2018? What were the conclusions of the check? How do you assess the security status of coal mines in the Silesian Voivodeship? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u5	You're welcome, Minister, to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Thank you for these questions and bringing up this case that we've been through a few weeks earlier. The whole rescue and drama on the mine Zofiówka resulted from a shock, actually from an earthquake, which is rare in this area as well as in mining. As far as the mining office is concerned, what you asked about, the labour safety supervisory authorities are set out in the provisions of the Geological and Mining Law and in the regulations on supervision and control of the movements of mining plants, and all types of mining, not just this coal. As far as 2017 is concerned, according to the state of 31 December, i.e. summing up the entire past year, checks from mining offices took place in 7533 underground mining plants, including 41 underground mining plants. At that time, there were 177 thousand employees. people in all mining - this is important, it is about the scale of people involved in this industry. So the scale of the natural controls that result from the current supervision of the Higher Mining Authority on safety... These figures show that there are quite a number of inspections of mining plants. The control activities of mining authorities were aimed at developing potential sources of risks in mining plants - what you asked - and taking preventive action, because it is crucial to anticipate potential undesirable events, in order to eliminate them, in particular by controlling the work carried out, eliminating the risks of such behaviour and controlling the organisation of work. In 1500 cases, the works were temporarily stopped due to inspections carried out in underground mining plants. But what does it mean to stop? At a time when there is control and there are comments, it is sometimes eliminated immediately during the inspection, there are some recommendations. In the event of a stay of up to one minute, for 5 minutes it shall be noted that such a interruption is present. We can say that, in general, what you saw in 2017, which was very positive for us during this difficult restructuring process... We have often engaged with great strength in order to organize organizational matters in mining, the construction of the Polish Mining Group, the functioning of Jastrzębska Węglowa Company. We managed to build a system in the field of rescue stations, also with the involvement of WUG, because we set up an appropriate team in 2016 to evaluate regulations. Three regulations have entered into force that have clarified certain behaviours by introducing new technologies, a new way of functioning in certain areas as regards safety. I'm talking about a significant drop in the number of different events and accidents that were out there, health damage, mining. This was very positive information for us - information from the report of the Higher Mining Authority. Later, as we know, there was an incident in the mine Borinia-Zofiówka-Jastrzęba, specifically in the Movement of Zofiówka, which somehow filled us all with sadness. With regard to the checks that were carried out in Zofiówka, as you rightly asked, 36 inspections were carried out in 2017 specifically in this mining plant, while within 5 months in 2018, and thus until the event, there were 10 controls. In view of certain comments, the following sanctions have been applied to those responsible: 56 educational measures provided for in the Code of Offences have been applied, so instruction has been given, warning has been given, 24 mandates have been imposed, as they have happened, and four times have been requested by the court to draw the consequences. During the five months of these checks, 15 educational measures have been applied, 13 criminal mandates have been imposed and a single application has been sent to the court. This applies to mining workers, as well as companies that operate mines on individual works. As far as the issue of regulation is concerned, as we assess the state of security in mining, we can only... Every event, even the smallest one, is always a matter of concern and makes us try to analyze it and learn from it. Our mining, even though we had this incident in Zofiówka, where the miners died, is safe mining compared to other countries. For information, I would like to say that immediately, even during the rescue operation - which is not unique, but it was also applied here - the President of the Higher Mining Authority appointed a committee. The committee is currently working on proposals for the event. Of course, this committee also evaluates the deposits that remained after this event, the accident, the earthquake, the shock. It is about where, in the future, we can operate safely in Sofiówka. All I'm saying is that the moment the event took place, the work at the Sophie mine was stopped for one day at all. Later in these fields, where the Higher Mining Authority signaled that work could be undertaken, it was allowed, but under full supervision. One thing that is very important: many aspects of this rescue operation were new things. The new thing was... [[♪ The bell ♪]] Just two more words, Marshal. The new thing was that there was always a president or vice-president of the Higher Mining Authority on this action. I must also stress very strongly that there was also the president of the rescue station personally with his deputy. These are, I think, things that first appeared. The point is, there was this involvement of the organs in the rescue operation itself. It is a matter of the head of the rescue operation, the director of the mine, but it should be noted that there were also representatives of the board. It's a new phenomenon that hasn't happened so far. There were chords that we could learn about from the media, get information. These are the elements that have emerged during this unfortunate event, I think, positive. But let's agree, it was an event that filled us all with pain. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! By amending the Law on Hunting Law, the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of July 2014 was enforced. As we know, the deadline set for implementation expired in January 2016. This judgment was an incentive for those opposing hunting to apply to administrative courts to exclude their property from the hunting circuit regime and any application by the court was examined in favour of the applicant. This judgment also indicated that there should be enhanced supervision by the minister of the Polish Łowiecki Union, and that the property of farmers should be taken into account. With regard to the implementation of this judgment, this law introduced, among other things, because this is not all, the possibility of submitting comments and opinions concerning the process of creating the division of voivodship into circuits or obtaining compensation in the event of the inclusion of real estate in the circuit, not excluding from borders and causing a significant limitation of the possibility of using. It also strengthened the supervision of the Polish Łowiecki Union and, as far as farmers are concerned, strengthened their position at the time of assessment of injury. So far this estimation has been done on the basis of: hunter and farmer and farmers have often accused themselves of being disadvantaged in the situation that they are always in a losing position in the collision with hunter. This is why the legislative process proposed that these estimates be made by the assessment team, which was to be composed of the circuit manager and the injured farmer. Here, during the work in the Sejm, a parliamentary amendment was introduced, which also included the chiefs in this team. It was at the request of the farmers themselves, who wanted to have someone on this team who they could see as a supportive person. Hence the lack of consultation that you are talking about. It was a parliamentary amendment, already incorporated during the work on the project in the Sejm. With regard to the problems arising from this, information about these problems reaches the Ministry, also reaches the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, also appeared at the meeting of the Joint Government and Local Government Commission as a request from representatives of municipalities to take account of some of the Councils who refuse to participate in the assessment, which often does not take place either this estimation or immediately appeals to the superior body. How many claims for damages have been made? I'll give you the answer to that question in writing. If you are asking about the Polish Hunting Union, how it refers to changes, then at this moment representatives of the hunting union were asked by us to report any problems that arise during estimation. On the part of hunters these problems are not reported at this time, on the part of farmers, yes. This is most often due to the fact that the chiefs, who should participate in the estimation, or refuse to participate and appoint no one in return, or even if they participate in the estimation, claim that they are not entitled to do so, that they do not have the opportunity to do so. Let me just remind you that the Act provides for the possibility of absence in the estimation of the representatives of the tenant or the circuit manager, and the absent farmer may also be the head of this team, while the representative of the municipality is the chairman. If the Chief doesn't want to estimate, he can't estimate, he has other tasks, because, of course, the bill doesn't clearly indicate that it's specifically this Chief, he can authorize any other person, another farmer, another person who could do it on his behalf. I think that in the case of many leaders who have other professional responsibilities, this is effectively implemented. However, what is most important - we have made such a declaration during the meeting of the Joint Government and Local Government Commission - is that we will work on amending the project in this regard. May 28th this year to the Sejm was submitted a parliamentary bill amending the Law on Hunting Law, which concerns the modification of the procedure for the estimation of damages. If this project is accepted by most Members, the representatives of the municipalities will be completely excluded from the assessment teams and will be replaced by representatives of the provincial agricultural advisory centres who will be able to participate in the work of the team. The chairman of this team, as indicated in the draft, is to be the circuit manager. Thank you.

This does not mean that these old or closed landfills still need not be monitored and should not be degassed, as is the case. If that's not the case, it's the right thing to do. Each storage site shall have its instructions for the storage site under which such measures are foreseen. The question raised by the RES Act is whether it is profitable for the entrepreneur. It's a question of certificates, etc. However, this is kind of a separate thing, and as far as safety is concerned, in every situation, as mentioned, we have mobile stations on site that measure pollution. When it comes to these fires that have been recently, very often this cloud of smoke and temperature have caused pollution to migrate into the upper layers of the atmosphere, where there has been dispersal. Our mobile stations did not indicate crossings, but firefighters worked in gas masks and special cameras at the site. So it was a local threat, but fortunately, based on the information from the modern equipment that the Environmental Protection Inspection has, we have not identified such threats to the surrounding population. As we look at it, this aggregation of unemployment data takes place at the level of the county. A lower level would require, I would say, the need for changes in regulations already of a systemic nature, because according to the District Government Act, the task of countering unemployment and activating the local labour market is dealt with by the district government. The best example here is the county labour offices. Therefore, the CSO also provides data at the level of the county rather than the municipalities. This is why we decided to assess the level of unemployment at the level of the county when awarding the location of individual investments in the Act, but this does not exclude other actions aimed at the level of the municipality. The Council's task will be to propose, to evaluate measures that could contribute to increasing the attractiveness of investment. Therefore, it will be an obligation, or perhaps more, to issue recommendations that can contribute to the investment attractiveness of municipalities. The list of these cities is annexed to the regulation and is therefore an integral part of this act. That is where investment will be awarded in counties with unemployment rates which exceed the average unemployment rate in the country. As far as road or rail investments are concerned, it is only one sentence that they are primarily public investments, and the new investment law concerns private investments, hence in this area. This organizational standard in diabetesology will be developed by the end of August. Here, on the other hand, is the procedure of verification by the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarification, resulting from the Act on the provision of health care financed by public funds. This protects us from introducing specific solutions that do not have a properly proven health effect. As part of this work, a broad team of experts dealing with basic health care is also included in the team. And here we want to prepare far more solutions at different levels of disease, which is also at the stage of identifying people who are at risk, who already have glycosylated haemoglobin levels close to this limit as a basis for identifying diabetes. These people we want to identify and appropriate preventive actions then carry out, which will also be financed by the NFZ, mainly within this organisational standard. Then we have a question of how to coordinate the care of a patient already diagnosed with diabetes, so that both pre-insulin treatment and treatment with insulin alone occur at an appropriate stage, at the beginning of symptoms, because medically speaking, complications of organs are the most difficult element in diabetes, e.g. limbs, blood system, vision and a number of others. So that here these elements are currently being developed. In August we will present this programme for public discussion, debate. I am counting on the involvement of people working in this team, that guidelines will be developed as soon as possible, so that both the POZ nurse, the family doctor, the diabetesologist in the diabetes clinic as well as the internists and the specialist medical examiners will know exactly how much different financial facilities can be used. This medicine prevents hypoglycaemia, a serious complication of diabetes. Currently, long-acting insulin analogues are refunded only when the patient has already had complications. This sometimes leads to dangerous and paradoxical situations in which desperate patients lead to such complications in their body to gain access to safer medicines. Hence the question: What Ministry plans to act in this respect and does it also consult patient organisations to meet their expectations? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u87	Thank you very much. And please respond to Minister Zbigniew Joseph the King.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u88	Thank you again, Marshal. Here, too, a lot more time would have to be spent on the refund of these insulin analogues, especially insulins that are medium-term and long-acting, because these are the elements from which to finance on the scale that is desirable, actually as a system we have problems. I'll start by working with patient organizations. Diabetes is indeed a disease that integrates local communities, and basically in most counties we have certain groups of people concentrated in thematic associations often, which as if they were dealing with it, and we meet with their representatives on an ongoing basis. This is a group that actually brings a lot from the perspective of the patient and his needs. This does not change the fact that the refund scheme is set at a certain level, 17% in the income of the NFZ. The allocation of these measures must therefore be appropriate. The reimbursement procedure in Poland is also known, because it is an entity that wants to distribute medicines, must submit a specific application supported by an assessment of clinical efficiency, i.e. safety of the therapies used, as well as two additional elements, i.e. economic analysis and an analysis of the impact on the budget. For several years we have had a problem especially with this third analysis, i.e. regarding this budgetary impact, which is simply often too big, simply impossible to finance. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As far as agricultural markets are concerned, this is the basic area that the resort has been engaged in for more than two years since the beginning of its term of office, because one of the basic objectives of our action and government programme is to ensure the profitability of agricultural production. It is well known that we have a liberalised world market, that these mechanisms that are being applied to us are often dependent, as well as the result of European Union law. You have to move in these areas. As far as the fruit and vegetables market is concerned, there is a very large number of products for which it is difficult to establish common rules for action. As early as 2016, the Resort adopted an action programme on nine basic agricultural markets, including fruit and vegetable markets. This programme included and includes those activities that have been and are being implemented. At the same time, for each of these markets and the general resort programme, the administration is working on specific solutions. We have recently adopted a programme for the reconstruction or development of the beef sector. Now we are intensively dealing with the fruit and vegetables market. In the programme adopted, we point to the possibility of using the European Union's funds, the Rural Development Programme, from the producer group. All these instruments have been introduced and applied. It's being realized. These basic objectives are to strengthen cooperation between fruit and vegetable producers - we are talking here about the creation of producer organisations - the dissemination of trade on the basis of contracts, the shortening of the marketing chain, i.e. the elimination of intermediary links in the trade in fruit and vegetables, the sale by farmers, the reduction of the cost of producing fruit and vegetables - here we are talking about these instruments from the RDP - or the proper implementation of market support instruments. These actions have been taken, they are functioning, but it is clear that there has been a crisis in agricultural markets at the turn of the year. And here at the initiative of the association, i.e. vegetable union in one of the districts or potato associations, we have taken and are taking action to stabilise the fruit and vegetables market. Already in 2016, we started negotiations with Brussels on the possibility of exporting potatoes, because this is one of the main problems with potatoes. After a year, i.e. On 26 April 2017, I issued a regulation on detailed measures to combat and prevent the spread of potato ring bacteriosis. At this point, in agreement with the potato association, we introduced facilitations in sending potatoes from Poland to other Member States of the European Union. First of all, it was possible to send potato tubers without having to be tested if they come from a holding recognised by the State Plant Protection and Seeding Inspection as a place of production free of bacteriosis. Secondly, in the case of the dispatch of potatoes to other Member States under the existing rules for testing for ring bacteriosis, 200 units are collected, not 400 tubers of each 25 t, as has been the case so far, which has doubled the costs of testing. The obligation to seal the means of transport to which potatoes are dispatched has been waived. In this respect, as far as export opportunities are concerned, this process is ongoing. At this stage, farms may be free, but if checks show that larger areas are free of this disease, larger areas will be released. The second problem to be addressed is the issue of imports, exports, especially imports from the countries of the European Union, because as far as countries outside the customs territory of the European Union are concerned, all parties are controlled, and if the parties are of inadequate quality, they are referred back. As far as Egypt is concerned, for example, where a sluice was found, such a potato disease, we applied to the European Commission, because this is the procedure, so that potatoes are not exported to us from this customs territory. After the meetings, because I had several meetings with associations or producers from some municipalities on this matter, we tightened the control, increased the number of checks. I just have to say here that these checks, when I am a minister, are being carried out all the time, and now there is a need for social expectations to increase their number. And here I will refer to the question of the Member in terms of inspections. This bill has been in Parliament for almost a year now. I don't know why she's not being processed, because one of the problems is that, ladies and gentlemen, the health inspection... ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u157	We proceed with the examination of item 29 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Code of Offences Act and some other laws (Print No 2557). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Marcin Warchoł to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u158	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The project involves changing the inefficient parametric system, which does not allow the effective prosecution of the perpetrators of minor offences against property, by introducing a rigid threshold of overfishing between the offence and the crime of acts against property for the amount of PLN 400. The current legal situation, I must say, probably a bit outrageous to the perpetrators of these offences, and this is nothing more than an incentive for the thief to commit such acts. The more a society gets rich, the more a thief can steal, risking only committing a crime. From an axiological point of view, it's very bad. Every year, the extent of social harm and the legal nature of the act change. This forces the courts, at the same time, to requalify these acts in the following year for a misdemeanor. So something that at the time of the theft was a crime - the perpetrator knew that he was committing a crime, the victim felt it as a crime - in the following year it becomes a crime. There's nothing more demoralizing. Finally, paradoxical situations occur when the perpetrator himself, e.g. delaying such conduct, it can effectively carry out its conduct, unethical conduct which postpones the process, lead to the result that it will be punished more gently - not for the offence at risk of imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years, but only for the offence. Here's another example. Two unsubs commit shoplifting on the same day. In the case of one of them, the case shall be opened in the year in which the act takes place and in the year following the other. It's completely different. The first one is a criminal, the second one only commits a misdemeanor. So that there are a lot of practical problems connected with the activity of justice, with the activity of courts, is the need to re-qualify these acts, and finally, as I said at the outset, it is simply immoral that the perpetrator who commits the crime in one year, in the next year for the same act, be judged as a misdemeanor. Please pay attention to the data of only one of the commercial networks. In July 2014, when the stealing threshold of PLN 420 was in force, there were 21 such events. At that time, goods were stolen for about 7 thousand. zł. A year later, when the threshold rose to PLN 437, the sum of losses and the number of thefts doubled. In July 2016, when the catch limit reached more than 462 PLN, there were 66 thefts for a total of 22 thousand. zł. We see, therefore, that the rolling value of this threshold results in a rolling increase in both theft and its values. The unsubs are calculating it. What's more, we know from the police's information that the perpetrators are spending a special fund on the organisation of mandates to pay the fines issued for such thefts. The police reveal a huge number of these kinds of thefts. They are a nightmare. I'm sure you see, you're in contact with the small shop owners, the people who run the family stores. They must involve property protection services, they must organise special third parties and the perpetrators are impunity. One day they steal in one shop, the other day - the other day, the day after - in another city. They're completely invisible to the justice system. That's why we're making a record of these types of offences. This is the second very important change. We introduce a fixed threshold of a half-life of 400 PLN, and the second very important change - we introduce a register of such offences and the police will already see, have statistics and subsequent offences committed will be shown in this register, and when the sum of these offences comes closer to a criminal act, then the perpetrator will be judged as a criminal. The third very important change is that theft in this case will be treated as a crime. In the event of the accumulation of two or more acts committed at short intervals, in the exercise of the pre-emptive intention of an exhaustive mark of the same offence, they will constitute a criminal offence in view of the total value of the property. These are three very important changes: the constant threshold of theft, the fixed threshold of the catch-up, first, in the place of the present crooked, which is, as I said, 1/4 of the average salary, second, the record of the offenses, thirdly, the creation of a crime where the sum of these values of the stolen property will already enter the area of crime. Further amendments are to extend the catalogue of criminal sanctions for criminal offences for restricting freedom in order to make the decision more flexible and more efficient. Ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo, this is of great importance when it comes to reducing cognition. In the present situation, when the perpetrator declares at the time of judgment or the court sees that the perpetrator is unable to pay the amount of the fine he is ordered, the court is not in a position to prosecute immediately in the form of a restriction of freedom through socially useful works. We must wait for the implementation of the enforcement procedure and only then do the same thing twice: first we decide, knowing that the fine in question will not be enforced and then we wait for the enforcement procedure and replace the fine on the basis of Article. 25 Code of Offences for Socially Useful Work. We're done with this unnecessary job, and we're back to judgment. The court will be able to flexibly see the property situation of the perpetrator, seeing his behaviour and attitude, immediately at the time of ruling the punishment of socially useful works. Other amendments also seek to relieve the court of unnecessary penalties in the case of offences. We are also introducing a legal regulation allowing the perpetrator to be free from the penalty of the restriction of freedom by returning to the fine. In the event of a fine being fined and then the fine cannot be enforced, the perpetrator does not pay it, there is a change in that fine either for detention or for socially useful work. As for the arrest, the perpetrator may return to the fine, he may be released from this arrest when he sees the ailment of this penalty when he sees the need to return to the fine, declare payment of the fine, he may return to it. However, when he is doing socially useful work, there is no such return. We are introducing an appropriate solution that will allow the unsub who works socially usefully to redeem himself and pay the fine that was originally convicted of him, but who did not pay it or declare that he would not pay it. Further amendments allow the police to apply coercive measures as part of their investigation of a suspected person. These are very important changes to equip the police with real means of intervention in such situations. Finally, the changes we are making are designed to improve the mandate procedure. First of all, we introduce deadlines, we harmonise in the article. 97 § 1 of the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters Time limits for the imposition of fines in the form of a criminal mandate and we standardise the start of that period from the time when the perpetrator of the offence was established. We clarify the elements to be included in the mandate: qualification, date, place of action. We are introducing the service of the perpetrator of the by-passing mandate next to the current form, i.e. leaving him in such a place that the perpetrator can pick him up immediately. It will be possible to effectively release the fine imposed in the form of a mandate by paying such a mandate at the police station, without the need to refer the case to court proceedings without further investigation. If a police officer sees that the perpetrator of the offence wishes to pay such a fine in the form of a mandate at the police station, it will be possible to close the case by now. This also serves to reduce the cognition, to free the courts from such cases, and I will remind you that there are about 0.5 million cases in courts. We extend from 7 to 14 days the time limit for payment by the perpetrator of the fine imposed by means of an eyewitness mandate and clarify that the penalty mandate should indicate the authority for which the fine may be paid within that time limit. We design that the law will enter into force within 14 days of the date of publication, except for the provisions concerning the register I mentioned. These shall enter into force 12 months after the date of the announcement. The solutions presented ensure effective prosecution and prosecution of perpetrators of property offences and improve criminal proceedings and, finally, standardise and systematic terminology and introduce legal solutions that are necessary to make it fairer and more efficient. Thank you very much.

We are therefore dealing with an uncontrolled change in the real value of the threshold. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u166	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club Modern I submit my position on the government draft law amending the Code of Offences Act and some other laws. In general, this project focuses on such a few basic issues. The first is the changes to the so-called regulation. half-deeds. Further issues concern the creation of a police record of offences, changes in the mandate procedure, tightening the rules on the holding of dangerous animals. In the current state of law, half-off actions constitute a misdemeanor if the value of things does not exceed 1/4 of the minimum wage, i.e. at present it is the amount of PLN 525. As the doctrine and case law show, the value of the property subject to the act should be assessed in relation to the time of the act and the time of judgment and not in relation to the time of judgment under Article. 4 § 2nd Penal Code. The way of regulating the half-deeds has long been discussed in doctrine. Depending on whether an act constitutes a criminal offence or an offence, the value of the subject matter of the enforcement action, while at the same time changing the threshold of that liability, depending on the amount of the minimum remuneration per year, was intended to achieve those values. This regulation, regardless of the level of the minimum remuneration, was therefore intended to fulfil a value-making role. The proposed change implies a return to the rigid limit of responsibility of PLN 400. This is the amount that was in 2013. It should also be noted that the amendment will qualify acts which under the current rules would be considered to be offences, which could create very important judicial problems. The project also provides for the introduction of additional powers for the Police in the form of the creation of a central record of offences, in fact a database of offenders, as well as suspected offenders and victims, without the knowledge and consent of both. Moreover, these data will also be processed and used preventively by other services. The scope of the data collected is also very wide and beyond the basic data for perpetrators and suspects includes, among others. information about the family, material and personal situation. Moreover, restrictions on the use of the register, the protection of the data contained therein, the manner and limits of their processing will only be determined by a regulation, i.e. an implementing act to the law. In our view, this type of legislation should be included in the bill. And here Mr Kukiz (15 mentioned GDPR. The question is, how does this register fit into these regulations? In addition, it is important to stress the adequacy of the collection and collection of these data to the situation of suspects, in relation to information on the family, material and personal situation. We talk all the time about the perpetrators, not the criminals, but the misdemeanors. The draft also includes changes to reduce the time limit for the imposition of mandates in the mandate procedure to 60 days. Until now, these deadlines were different - 14, 90, 180 days respectively - depending on how the perpetrator was exposed. However, it should be noted that when the perpetrator of the offence was caught in the act of act or immediately after the offence was committed, that date was 14 days. The extension of up to 60 days in this respect should be assessed strongly negatively. The rules on the holding of dangerous animals are tightened, which, in the light of reports of how many situations we have in this regard, cases of improper handling of dangerous animals, should be considered as a positive change. On the other hand, there are less restrictive provisions on employment offences, including illegal work by foreigners. [[♪ The bell ♪]] There is a waiver of the minimum fine. I'm almost done. This seems to be a response to labour market demand, including seasonal work. However, it should be pointed out that solutions should be more systematic in this respect. Thank you. The Modern Posel Club requests that the bill be submitted to the committees for further work. [[Applause]] [[Very substantive speech. Congratulations.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u184	Now, Marshal. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Act on Combating Unfair Competition and some other laws, Parliamentary Printing No. 2549. First, the purpose of the proposed law is to transpose into Polish law Directive 2016/943 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 8 June 2016 on the protection of classified know-how and classified commercial information, business secrets, against their unlawful acquisition, use and disclosure. The current rules of national legal order in the Member States of the Union create an unequal level of protection of company secrecy. These differences may discourage entrepreneurs from taking up activities of an international nature, especially in an area requiring investment in innovation. The solutions provided for in the draft law will ensure the minimum standard for the protection of business secrets required by the provisions of Directive 2016/943. The project implementing this objective includes a proposal, inter alia. amendments to the Act of 16 April 1993 on combating unfair competition and the Act of 17 November 1964. Code of Civil Procedure. The proposed solutions include clarification of the definition of company secrecy, an example of unfair competition in breach of company secrecy and an indication of circumstances excluding liability in the event of breach of company secrecy. The proposed law also introduces new measures to protect the company's secrecy, which will be applied in civil proceedings in the event of a breach of that secret. These include the publication of a judgment or information on its content, the settlement of remuneration for the use of the company's secrecy in the future and the determination of compensation as the equivalent of the licence fee due for the exercise of the company's secrecy. This last solution refers to the regulation already existing in the Polish legal system in the Copyright Law and Related Rights Act and in the Industrial Property Law Act. These arrangements have been designed to ensure that the interests of the parties to the process are balanced and to provide the court with a flexible tool in the case of breach of business secrecy. The Act will also regulate the question of the protection of the company's secrecy in connection with legal proceedings relating to its infringement. If a participant in a trial concerning a breach of the secret of an undertaking, excluding disclosure, discloses or uses such confidential information as he has received during that process, such action shall be subject to criminal liability. This applies to all participants in the trial, not only to the parties, but also to representatives, experts or judges. I hope that the solutions proposed will ensure a high level of protection of business secrecy in the common market, which should, on the one hand, discourage unfair competition and, on the other hand, create a transparent and stable legal framework for the use of know-how. The proposed standard for the protection of business secrecy will make the market more competitive. I ask the High House to proceed smoothly on the project, bearing in mind that it is fulfilling the obligations arising from Poland's membership of the European Union. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u185	Thank you very much, Minister. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u186	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion on Draft Print Act No. 2549 amending the Act on Combating Unfair Competition and some other laws. The purpose of the proposed law is to transpose into Polish law Directive (EU) 2016/943 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 8 June 2016. Directive 2016/943 of the European Union is a summary of nearly five years of work by European Union bodies to harmonise rules on standards for the protection of confidential information of an undertaking, i.e. classified know-how and classified commercial information, against their unlawful acquisition, use and disclosure. This Directive provides for a minimum harmonisation model, with a number of exceptions contained in several articles of the Directive, with a view to fully harmonising legislation on this issue between the Member States of the European Union, inter alia. as regards the definition of lawful acquisition, use and disclosure of business secrets, circumstances in which the trader does not obtain legal protection despite the acquisition, disclosure or use of information which is a secret of his business, or the limitation period for claims for unlawful activities relating to business secrecy. However, the minimum harmonisation model adopted by the European Union legislator in the implementation of the Directive means that the level of protection of information which is a secret of an undertaking in the laws of individual Member States must not be lower than that laid down in Directive 2016/943. Art. 114 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, which constitutes the legal basis for this Directive, provides for the adoption of EU rules harmonising national rules, since the national rules currently in force in the Member States create an unequal level of protection of business secrets within the European Union, and it is necessary to ensure a properly functioning internal market throughout the Union. The current legal situation in Poland in the areas covered by Directive 2016/943 of the European Union deviates from the standard contained in the Directive, which forces the need for legislative intervention in several legislative acts. We refer to the following acts: the Act of 16 April 1993 on combating unfair competition, the Law of 17 November 1964. The Code of Civil Procedure and the Act of 16 February 2017 on the protection of competition and consumers. In order to transpose Directive 2016/943 of the European Union, the draft in print No 2549 has just been proposed to amend the abovementioned laws. According to Art. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Directive 2016/943 of the European Union should be transposed by the Member States by 9 June 2018, so that there is little time for the adoption of that law. The proposed law does not require notification to the European Commission under the Act of 30 April 2014 on State aid proceedings and is, of course, compatible with European Union law. Important information is that this regulation is beneficial for the small and medium-sized enterprises sector. It ensures a high standard of protection of information relevant for the acquisition and maintenance of customers by small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as ensuring sound competition mechanisms. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the adoption of a draft law on printing [[♪ The bell ♪]] No 2549 and proposes to refer it to further work in committee. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u191	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u192	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party - the European Union of Democrats, I will present my position on the Government's draft print law No. 2549. As has already been said, the aim of the project is to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the protection of classified know-how, classified commercial information against their unlawful acquisition, use and disclosure. The Directive establishes a minimum standard for the protection of confidential business information in the management of both competitiveness and innovation. Thus, it harmonises the protection of business secrets throughout the European Union and allows, as a matter of fact, to secure a cross-border economic activity. The implementation of the Directive under our conditions concerns the amendment of three legal acts. Now I'm going to refer to them one by one. The first is an amendment to the Act on Combating Unfair Competition. These changes concern, among others, clarify the definition of the company itself and the definition of unfair competition. The definition of unfair competition has been extended to include the so-called "unfair competition'. other information which is a secret to the undertaking with regard to its disclosure, use and acquisition. In principle, the draft reflects the definition contained in the directive that we are implementing. Similarly, the definition of company secrecy is adapted to the definition contained in the Directive. And so on. The directive does not contain any time limits for this protection and will therefore be withdrawn from our legal system, which has so far been required by a company employee, for example. to keep the company secret for a period of 3 years from the date of termination of employment. In addition, the proposed changes increase the scope of claims for the entitled in the event of unfair competition, including inter alia. making public e.g. a judgment or a claim for compensation in the form of payment to the claimant of the appropriate remuneration. The amendments also eliminate the three-year limitation period for damages claims, extending it to up to 20 years in the case of unfair competition which is a crime or offence at the same time, as well as the extension of liability to persons who have taken information, e.g. in the course of legal proceedings. This will then apply to parties to proceedings, representatives, experts, translators, minutesmen, as well as persons who otherwise had access to files, e.g. for visits or administrative supervision, research or scientific work. The second law which is subject to amendment is the Code of Civil Procedure. In fact, here these changes are in the scope of security proceedings. Once the provisions of the Directive have been introduced, there will be an extension of the bail-in institutions. A circle of people entitled to pay bail in advance of other charges will be extended immediately after enforcement costs. Protection of third parties shall be introduced in the event that they suffer losses as a result of the provision. Similarly, in the case of the last Act on Competition and Consumer Protection: here these amendments are purely technical, relate to the same definition of company secrecy in both the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and the Act on Combating Unfair Competition. In conclusion, it should be considered that the changes are very precise and extensive in their content. As we can hear, they must be taken within a reasonable period of time. I believe that it is worth noting that the government has addressed this proposal for consultation, [[♪ The bell ♪]] so he was consulted. There's been a lot of conclusions. I think they will still be subject to an in-depth analysis. So that my club is in favour of directing the project to further work in Parliament. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u193	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mr Ireneusz Gains, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u194	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on the fight against unfair competition and certain other laws. The draft law submitted is a transposition into the Polish legal order of the EU Parliament and Council Directive 2016/943 of 8 June 2016 on the protection of classified know-how and classified commercial information (the secrets of the enterprise) against their unlawful acquisition, use and disclosure. The main objective of the Directive was to establish a minimum standard for the protection of confidential information by companies as a tool for managing competitiveness and innovation. In a globalised world, in the environment of dynamically developing technologies, the risk that people who have failed to obtain information that is a secret of the company has become serious as never before. It is therefore necessary to develop legal tools that will enable entrepreneurs to protect key information about their activities, in particular by unambiguously sanctioning as an act of unfair competition attempts to obtain, use or disclose such information. It is worth highlighting the long, more than 90-year history of legislation relating to the fight against unfair competition. It found its origin in the Act of 2 August 1926 on combating unfair competition, in which it was Article. 10 was about sanctioning the unlawful exploitation of the company's secret. Given that the subject matter of the directive is regulated, i.e. Whereas the concept of unlawful action in relation to business secrecy as an act of unfair competition has been the subject of the Act of 16 April 1993 on combating unfair competition for years, it is appropriate for the applicant to propose that the directive be implemented by amending that law rather than creating a completely new, separate one. The proposal also introduces to a lesser extent amendments to the Act of 17 November 1964. The Code of Civil Procedure and the Act of 16 February 2007 on Competition and Consumer Protection. To discuss the most important changes briefly, it should be pointed out that due to discrepancies it was necessary to adapt the Polish definition of company secrecy to the definition contained in the article. Point 2 (1) of the Directive. In accordance with the new definition set out in Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Act on the fight against unfair competition: by the secret of an undertaking, technical, technological, organisational or other information of economic value is understood, which as a whole or in a specific combination and a set of their elements are not generally known to persons normally involved in this type of information or are not readily accessible to such persons, provided that they are entitled to use or dispose of the information they have taken, with due care, to ensure that they are kept confidential. In this context, it is very useful for entrepreneurs to add a solution that allows a judgment against the defendant, i.e. the perpetrator of unfair competition relating to the secret of the undertaking, ordering the publication of a judgment or information on the content indicated on an equal footing with other alternative: a request for payment of the appropriate remuneration, compensation or compensation of the damage caused. This solution was introduced by adding paragraph in the Act. Article 3 18: In the event of a judgment by the court of such an order, and subsequently of its default by the defendant on a voluntary basis, the execution will be carried out in accordance with the provisions of the law of K.p.c. on the enforcement of non-monetary benefits. In conclusion, the implementation of the provisions of Directive 2016/943 by amending the provisions of the Act on Combating Unfair Competition underlines the importance of protecting business secrets for the development of Polish companies and maintaining proper competition in the market. This change should be particularly beneficial for small and medium-sized enterprises, for which good legal conditions ensuring the protection of key information that is a secret of the company are of particular importance for the acquisition and maintenance of customers with a view to further expansion. The implementation of the Directive should also have a positive impact on the development of competition mechanisms, which is under the supervision of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle requests that the bill be submitted to the committees for further work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u199	Thank you very much, Member. I ask Minister Luke Piebiak to answer questions. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u200	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I will answer briefly the questions asked by your Members. With regard to the question of Mr Wilczyński from the Platform regarding the actions to be taken and the principles that certain measures must be taken in order to preserve the company's secrecy, of course how this will ultimately look will depend on how judicial case-law will be shaped. It seems that the project is drawn up as it should be, flexibly, because it has no reason to protect something that the entrepreneur does not consider to be a secret, which he considers absolutely revealing, does nothing to protect certain information. Since ancient times, however, there has been a general principle that no harm has been done to a willing person. If someone thinks he can disclose specific information, it is difficult for the legislator here to take action against the will of the directly interested person. Of course, the way of protecting this information will probably vary depending on what information it is, what kind of business it is, whether it is a big businessman or a small businessman. All these situations such a general formula of the provision seems to take into account. And as for the details, if there is court proceedings and a dispute over whether the trader has taken certain measures to keep certain information secret or not, then the court will have to... The circumstances of a particular case will be dealt with in court proceedings, the parties will speak out, the court will have to assess and deliver a judgment on the basis of such a general provision. But general clauses are not unusual, they are a normal thing in the legal system, and yet they play an important role in the legal system, because we cannot create law on a casuistic basis. We wouldn't be able to imagine all the situations that might exist anyway. So that's what court proceedings are for, and that's why there are parties who convince the court to give a fair ruling on the basis of rules that are, by nature, general. With regard to Mrs Malik's question, where does this 3-year limitation, which has been and which is now not going to be, in line with the draft, and how it will look in time to protect the interests of the trader, the interests in the form of keeping certain data as confidential - first of all, the Directive. The Directive does not in any way allow for the limitation of the period during which a person is obliged to comply with this secrecy, the non-disclosure of information, in this case the worker. So this 3-year limitation, which exists today, had to be removed, because otherwise we would have been immediately justified, as it seems at first glance, by the allegation that we are not implementing the directive as it should be. Besides, there is no particular reason to release a worker after 3 years from responsibility for an action that can cause powerful damage - powerful damage, sometimes leading to bankruptcy of a company, liquidation, dismissal of employees, etc., because knowledge, confidential information is often essential if not only capital, especially entrepreneurs operating in innovative markets, which are, say, quite unprecedented activities. This mystery often determines the existence of an entrepreneur and there is no rational reason to say: after 3 years it can be revealed. So this law protects the secrets of the company more than it is in the present state. As it seems, this is in no way disputed by the High House. This is the right direction, and this is in line with the directive that we have to implement since we are a member of the European Union. As regards measures aimed at extorting a specific action, these are financial sanctions or the publication of a judgment. This was specifically foreseen in the project. This is a consequence of the directive, because this is the starting point that we had to preserve. By the way, I would like to end by thanking all the representatives of the clubs for having responded positively to this project prepared by the Ministry of Justice, and apologizing for the fact that it is in June and the deadline for implementation is 9 June, but also explaining what this results from. This is due to what has been seen - for which I thank you - by the honourable Members and included in their statements: we wanted to consult as much as possible. We even analyzed the comments submitted after the deadline and tried to take them into account so that no one could say that we missed something, that we were in too much of a hurry. If we fail to implement the deadline, we will be a month or two late, then the tragedy will not be, but the project is to be good. As late as April, we had received comments from the river community, and already in May we presented a project to Parliament. The Patent Office of the Republic of Poland and the Polish Chamber of Patent Ombudsmen submitted these comments quite late, but we considered that it was better to analyse them and present to the High House a decent project for which all clubs will be able to take a positive position rather than to present to the High House something that is not fully refined. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This is a clear correction of his previous position as a pro-Western party, which has even submitted a draft law to abandon Russian integration structures such as the Eurasian Economic Union by Armenia. It is understandable that Armenia is heavily dependent on Russia, which has numerous political, military and economic instruments to influence the Armenian authorities. Therefore, it is absolutely right to strive for the European Union, including Poland, to strengthen and deepen cooperation and partnership with Armenia as defined by the agreement we are considering. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports the bill and will vote in favour of it. Thank you.

In accordance with the obligation under Article. 4 Act on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners I would like to present to you the report of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration on the implementation in 2017 of the Act on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners. It contains an analysis of the subject matter and presents the scale of interest of foreigners in acquiring real estate in 2017 as well as shares and shares in companies owned or owned by perpetual real estate users in Poland. The data contained in the report reflect the number of decisions issued by the Minister in 2017 for the purchase by foreigners of real estate, shares or shares, as well as the scale acquired by aliens of real estate, shares and shares which illustrate the Minister's record of purchased real estate, shares and shares by permission and without the required permission. The year 2017 was the first reporting year when the amended law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners was in force. I would like to point out that 26 355 documents have been received by the Ministry this year, which, compared to 2016, resulted in an almost 57 percent increase in the number of documents. The main objective of the amendment to the Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners was to build a new system for obtaining documents and information on transactions involving foreigners, in particular concerning capital transactions, so as to obtain the same information from different sources wherever possible. On the occasion of this amendment, the scope of the information obligation to notaries has also been extended, taking into account new documents which the Act has not yet provided for. In addition, public administrations are included in the catalogue of obliged entities. At this point, I would like to stress that the records of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration in 2017 were drawn up on the basis of: extracts of notarial acts and copies of contracts with notarially certified signatures, extracts of certificates of inheritance together with extracts of inheritance protocols, copies of European certificates of succession, if they were found to have been inherited by the foreigner, copies of final decisions on the acquisition of the inheritance and copies of documents submitted to the register files, which show that there was an acquisition or acceptance of shares, shares or general rights and obligations by the foreigner in a commercial company which is the owner or perpetual user of real estate located in the territory of the Republic of Poland. In view of the above, the completeness of the records kept by the Minister depends to a large extent on the reliability of the statutory obligation to send the documents to the Minister by notaries and courts. To this end, together with the Ministry of Justice and the National Chamber of Notarial Affairs, we are taking appropriate action. As in previous years, there are a small number of permits issued for the acquisition, acquisition of shares or shares in companies owned by or users of perpetual real estate located in Poland, because capital turnover for entities originating in the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation is free and free to do so. In accordance with the law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners, in 2017 the Minister responsible for Home Affairs issued 224 permits for the acquisition of 270 land, including 28 permits for the acquisition of agricultural and forestry property, 80 permits for the acquisition of 93 residential and commercial premises, 19 permits for the acquisition of shares and shares in companies owned or used for the perpetual property in Poland with an area of 88 ha. The permits concerned mostly properties located in Mazowieckie, Pomorskie, Zachodniopomorskie and Lubuskie voivodships. The authorisation holders were dominated by entities with or representing the capital of Ukraine and Belarus. Last year, the Home Minister issued 13 decisions refusing foreigners permission to acquire land, premises, shares and shares. Within this number, negative decisions were issued because the foreigner failed to show his permanent relationship with Poland or not to comply with the statutory conditions justifying the issue of the permit. Within the framework of all refusals, one decision was issued because of the opposition of the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development. The Minister of Home Affairs and Administration shall also undertake activities within the framework of the statutory powers to examine the legality of the acquisition by foreigners of immovable property, shares or shares. Where it is found that the transaction has been concluded in accordance with the provisions of the Foreign Property Acquisition Act, an entry in the Register of real estate acquired by foreigners shall be made with or without authorisation. As in recent years, most buyers have been citizens and entrepreneurs from the European Economic Area. The interest of foreigners in Polish real estate trading is constant. According to the data collected in the registers it appears that foreigners purchased land with a total area of 4483 ha on the basis of registered transactions, including agricultural and forestry property with a total area of more than 1 000, both on the basis of the required permission and without the obligation to apply for authorisation in 2017. ha based on 772 registered transactions, residential and utility premises with a total area of 754 998 m2 based on 6385 transactions. 91 acquisitions of shares or shares were registered in companies owned or perpetually owned by real estate with a total area of 2478 ha. In 2017, the largest number of real estate foreigners purchased in Mazowieckie, Pomeranian and Lower Silesian voivodships, and among the buyers dominated entities with nationality or representing the capital of Germany, the Netherlands and Austria. Given that the authorisation is valid 2 years from its date of issue and only the parties to the transaction depend on the conclusion of the contract, the data on the number of authorisations in the calendar year concerned do not correspond to the data on the real estate acquisition transactions made on the basis of the permit. Moreover, not every permit means the actual acquisition of the property by the foreigner, because the parties to the transaction may waive the intention to conclude the contract. In accordance with the Article. 6 Act to acquire property by a foreigner contrary to the provisions of the Act is invalid, and the annulment of the acquisition is ruled by the court at the request of the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, but this right holds the competent local authority, the governor, the Marshal of the voivodeship or the voivodeship. In 2017, 129 transactions entered into in breach of the law were recorded. Among these cases, in 72 cases, as a result of the actions taken by the Minister responsible for the internal affairs of the parties to the transaction, they themselves led to compliance with the law on the acquisition of immovable property by foreigners, while in the remaining 59 cases the Minister referred to the courts for annulment of the acquisition of immovable property by law. In conclusion, it should be noted that the revised provisions of the Law on the Acquisition of Real Estate by Aliens in force from 1 January 2017 have sealed and created a system for obtaining documents by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration to monitor trends in the acquisition of real estate, shares and shares by foreigners and to analyse the scale of interest of individual groups of entities, individuals and entrepreneurs representing foreign capital, the acquisition of specific types of real estate and the degree of interest in such real estate in individual voivodships. Therefore, the purpose of the amendment of the Law on the acquisition of real estate by foreigners and certain other laws of 2 December 2017 was fulfilled. The records are a database containing information on the property acquired by foreigners and the data of the companies in which foreign nationals acquired or acquired shares. These data are primarily derived from notarial acts sent, court orders and are obtained in the course of investigations carried out by the Authority. It should be noted that it is very important to ensure that the documents relating to the acquisition by foreigners of real estate, shares and shares are fairly transmitted by courts and notaries so that the records kept by the Minister contain complete data in this regard. It also requires that, despite the completion of all the transitional periods which were required by authorisation, namely the property acquired by foreigners from the European Economic Area and the Swiss Confederation, the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration is still the authority empowered to monitor the trading of immovable property with the participation of foreigners by keeping a register of immovable property, shares and shares acquired or covered by aliens. As already mentioned, the Act of 2 December came into force on 1 January. The key changes are the extension of the catalogue sent by the notary and the courts of documents, the inclusion of all documents resulting from notarial activities and any documents submitted to the register files resulting in the acquisition of real estate. The extension of the above catalogue resulted in a significant inflow, the impact of documents to the Minister, i.e. from 15 151 in 2016 to 26 355 in 2017, representing an increase of 57% documents which must be examined in substance. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-07-sejm-63-3.u262	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! In order to comply with the obligation under Article. 4th Act on the National Judicial Council I would like to present information from the activities of the National Council in 2017. This is the duration of the Council's previous composition and under a different leadership, so my report will be strictly formal and will rather be deprived of assessments of the Council's activities during the 2017 period. This relevant information, which includes a large number of figures, statistics, will allow me to anticipate the short introduction of legislative changes that have led to the structure and composition of the Council and the way it is set up in a new form, which will, of course, be reflected in the next year's report. These changes made in 2017, which were started in around May 2017, and completed in December 2017, had an impact on the functioning of the Council in 2017. These different legislative changes were an expression of the changing concepts of shaping the Council and how it was set up, aimed generally at increasing social control of the justice system in such a way that the composition of the part of the Council - i.e. 15 judges included in it - was appointed by the Sejm and not, as has been the case so far, by the judiciary. This first law was the Act of 11 May 2017, which limited the competence of the Council to appoint assesors only to the possibility of objecting. In addition, the number of members of the Board appointed to the Programme Council of the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office has been reduced, while broadening the competence to assess the appeal of the Director of the Office. Previously, this competence was limited to the opinion of his vocation. It also introduced the possibility, or actually the obligation to include the electronic system in the review process of judges, which was intended to improve the functioning of the Council and to develop this legal provision in the Rules of Procedure which was adopted in September 2017. The main stage of the amendment was opened by a judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 20 June 2017, in which the Constitutional Court challenged two essential provisions: one concerning the rules for the selection of judges to the National Courts Council, and the other concerning the understanding of the concept of term of office, i.e. the joint term of office and not the individual term of office. The development of these assessments by the Constitutional Tribunal was reflected in the draft two laws of 12 July 2017. One of them was an amendment to the Act on the National Judicial Council, the other was a law on the system of common courts. This first law granted the Sejm the right to elect 15 judges, provided for the termination by law of the employment relationship with the staff of the National Council's office and introduced an internal system of council consisting of the existence of two assemblies, which were to proceed in a quite complicated way due to the different weight and much voting or votes assigned to them. The broader development of this law is unnecessary because the President vetoed the law, it did not enter into force, and the second law I mentioned entered into force, which provided for two issues: one of fundamental importance, the other of secondary importance. In particular, the National Council was removed from the procedure for the appointment and dismissal of the Presidents and Vice-Presidents of the Courts and provided for such an information obligation to the Minister of Justice in the event of a request for the return of the Judge to the judicial position after having submitted a mandate, for example. as a result of work in an international organisation. Finally, the state-of-the-art structure of the National Council was shaped by the Act of 8 December 2017 following the President's proposal for an amendment, which was a simple consequence of his previous veto. This law was dealt with by two fundamental systemic issues and three procedural issues, namely the right to elect 15 members of the national council granted to the Sejm, the adoption of a joint term of four years of this part of the judicial national council, while the procedural issues were in line with the requests to increase the transparency of the Council's operation, to improve the procedure before it and to introduce the obligation to broadcast board meetings via the Internet, to vote and adopt resolutions on a circular basis and to ask the Minister for Justice's opinion on each individual case. As far as action is concerned, the Board carried out its duties through its bodies, as well as through standing committees of four, problem committees, of which five were. The composition of the National Council in 2017 expressed certain trends that had been revealed, namely that one mandate had not been filled after the expiry of the mandate of Judge Prenety-Ambicka in November 2017. Failure to set this mandate was an expression of a certain temporary expectation of change, because the Minister of Justice did not initiate the procedure for filling this mandate in this case. On the other hand, it was worth noticing that 9 of the 15 judges in this 2017 composition repeated their terms of office, they were already the second terms of office, which was an expression of the duration of the council just in a specific composition. The spokesperson of the national council was Judge Waldemar Żurek, and the deputy was Sławomir Stick. The members of the National Council took part in the work of certain external actors. I have already mentioned the Programme Council of the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office. Here, over the year 2017, there were three warehouses. Finally, the last one was Judge Dariusz Zawistowski in the implementation of this amended provision providing for the participation of only one person from the National Council in the composition of the Programme Council. In addition, Judge Wojtaszek took part in the judge's application contest, the judge Ewa Preneta-Ambick - in the judge's examination committee. Here, the competence of the board has also changed, because the powers of such an active member of the examination committee have only been brought into the role of observer, i.e. this competence has been changed in order to distinguish the stage of evaluation of the candidate, or as if in the process of acquisition of qualifications by the candidate, and later in the process of evaluating him as a whole in the board. One additional, although it may be said, secondary competence was to take part in the ruling committee in cases of violation of public finance discipline. Here, the participation of board members is optional. Basic statistics showing numerically the effects of the Council's work in 2017 are reduced to the following basic data. The Council held 18 plenary sessions lasting 57 days, presented 99 people to the President with requests for appointment to judge positions, 464 opinion groups were set up, which also took place, in addition to the examination of source material, i.e. these various opinions, assessment of visitors, meetings with candidates, court presidents, heads of departments and visitors. These meetings were held at 110. Compared to 2015 and 2016, it can be noted that the number of meetings and the number of days of these meetings are virtually identical. The number of persons proposed for appointment has been reduced almost four times, which was due to the fact that the procedure for filling posts was not initiated, i.e. there were no announcements, which involved inter alia. except that it was as if there were a pool of seats for graduates or assesors, i.e. graduates of the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office, who applied for the positions of assesors. An important measure in the work of the council, such as: The Supreme Audit Chamber shall be used to assess the effectiveness and effectiveness of the work of the various institutions, including the national council, as a measure of the duration of the opinion-making procedure. In 2015 it was 65 days, in 2016 - 66 days, and in 2017 - 54 days, which indicates high efficiency. I ignore the tables that are contained in the report, both the short ones and the full ones, because these are already very detailed data and their presentation or attempt to discuss them will not provide any relevant information. I will only stop at the evaluation of this final stage of the nomination procedures, because they assume an appeal to the Supreme Court. 35 such appeals were brought to the Supreme Court. The Court of First Instance recognised 34 cases in substance, repealed the resolution of the national council in 11 cases. This gives the efficiency or stability of these resolutions at 65% and this should be assessed as high stability. An important episode at the council's work was the issue of appointment to the position of assessors. Speaking of episodes, I do not intend to diminish the importance of this work or this part of the activity. He was so important that he aroused a lot of public interest. This part of the activity was opened with a summary list of requests to entrust judicial assistants with the duties of a judge. This was received on 13 October 2017, but due to the controversy between the Council and the Ministry of Justice regarding the completeness of documents allowing the position to be taken [[♪ The bell ♪]] - in particular, the lack of access to the IT system and insufficient documents in paper form - on 30 October 2017, the Council adopted 265 resolutions in secret voting. Later, due to these controversys and criticisms expressed by the media, the public opinion returned to this issue again and, following the re-examination of the matter by resolutions of 17 November, repealed the objections in 252 cases, and in eight cases refused to repeal those resolutions. They were later referred to by the Supreme Court and some of these appeals are recognised. One of the last decisions of the Supreme Court came to pass a few days ago. In general, the direction of the assessments that can be read from the justification of the Supreme Court's position is such that the assessments of these candidates should be dealt with in a less rigorous, less formalistic way. It was, among other things. the issue of integrity of character, which by the council was questioned with a calling for it...
